Yeah, definitely. That’s actually been one of the weirder parts, because I’m not someone who usually has a hard time filling my time. I used to really look forward to going for a run in the morning — nothing intense, just getting out before the day started, clearing my head a little. Lately I’ll set my shoes out or think, “Okay, tomorrow I’m going,” and then when the time comes it just feels like this huge chore, like I can’t make myself care enough to do it.

Same with playing guitar. I’ve always kept it nearby, and normally I’d pick it up without even thinking about it, just mess around for a while. Now it kind of just sits there. I look at it and think I should play, but there’s no pull toward it, if that makes sense.

And cooking dinner too — I used to actually enjoy that, planning what I was going to make, getting it started after work. Now it’s like every step feels like too much, and not in a busy way, more like… I just don’t get anything out of it. It all feels like another task I’m supposed to force myself through.

Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d use the word hopeless exactly every time, but most days there’s this heavy, flat feeling that I’m carrying around. I haven’t made any plans to hurt myself or anything like that. I don’t… I don’t want to scare anybody by saying it wrong.

But yeah, I have had thoughts where I think, like, if I just didn’t wake up in the morning, maybe that wouldn’t be the worst thing. Not that I’m trying to make that happen, and I’m not sitting there figuring out how to do something to myself. It’s more like when I’m really tired of pushing through the day, this thought comes in that maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t have to keep doing this.

It’s hard to admit that out loud, because I don’t want it to sound like I’m about to do something. I’m not. But the thought has been there.

It’s more like… even when I’m home with my family, or I’m sitting in the break room at work and people are talking, I’m there physically, but I don’t really feel there. Lately I’ll set my shoes out or think, “Okay, tomorrow I’m going,” and then when the time comes it just feels like this huge chore, like I can’t make myself care enough to do it.\n\nSame with playing guitar. I’ve always kept it nearby, and normally I’d pick it up without even thinking about it, just mess around for a while. Now it kind of just sits there. I don’t know exactly how many pounds, because I haven’t really been checking the scale every day or anything, but I wouldn’t be surprised if I have. The bigger thing I’ve noticed is my appetite’s been really off these last few weeks.

I’m not really sure how to answer that in a neat way. I haven’t been keeping track of it like, “I slept this many hours,” or anything. Some nights I’m in bed and it feels like I’ve been lying there a long time, but then I don’t know if I actually slept more than I think I did or less than I think I did.

The bigger thing I’ve noticed is my appetite’s been really off these last few weeks.\n\nThat’s actually one of the reasons I came in. I’ll get home after a shift and there’ll be food I normally like, stuff I’d usually have no problem eating, and it just kind of sits there in front of me. I’ll take a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it feels like I’m forcing it down more than actually wanting it. So maybe I’ve lost some weight, yeah, but mostly I’ve just barely been eating like I normally do.\n\nDoctor: How have you been sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that in a neat way. I haven’t been keeping track of it like, “I slept this many hours,” or anything. Some nights I’m in bed and it feels like I’ve been lying there a long time, but then I don’t know if I actually slept more than I think I did or less than I think I did.\n\nI guess what I can say is it hasn’t felt very restful. I’ll go to bed because I know I need to, especially with work and everything, but when morning comes I don’t always feel like I got much out of it. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, I’m still going in. I haven’t stopped showing up for work or anything, and at home I’m still doing the basic things I have to do, even if it takes me longer or I kind of drag myself through it.

But at work, it’s starting to show in ways that really bother me. My job is the kind where you’re supposed to be paying attention, writing things down correctly, catching things when they happen. And I’ve made some documentation mistakes lately that I normally wouldn’t make. Not huge, dramatic mistakes, but enough that someone noticed. And there was one surveillance cue I missed, which… that’s not like me. I keep replaying it because I know better.

I actually got a formal warning, and that’s probably what made me realize this isn’t just me having a rough few days. I’ve never really had that happen before. I’m usually dependable, I do the job, I keep my head down. So yeah, I’m functioning, technically, but it’s affecting my work now in a way it never used to.

I’m not sure. I mean, I’ve had bad stretches before, like anyone, where I was down for a few days or stressed out about work or money or whatever was going on at the time. But I don’t know if I’d say it was like this.

I’m trying to think back and not exaggerate either way. There were times where I wasn’t in a great place, but usually I could point to a reason and it would pass, or I’d still feel like myself underneath it. This feels more… I don’t know, more stuck, I guess.

So I don’t think I’ve had a period exactly like this before, not that I can clearly remember. But I also haven’t really sat down and thought about it in this kind of way before, so I’m not completely sure.

Some nights I’m in bed and it feels like I’ve been lying there a long time, but then I don’t know if I actually slept more than I think I did or less than I think I did.\n\nI guess what I can say is it hasn’t felt very restful. I’ll go to bed because I know I need to, especially with work and everything, but when morning comes I don’t always feel like I got much out of it. But I can’t say exactly how many hours, or whether it’s every single night. It’s just been… off, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still going in. I haven’t stopped showing up for work or anything, and at home I’m still doing the basic things I have to do, even if it takes me longer or I kind of drag myself through it.\n\nBut at work, it’s starting to show in ways that really bother me. My job is the kind where you’re supposed to be paying attention, writing things down correctly, catching things when they happen. And I’ve made some documentation mistakes lately that I normally wouldn’t make. Not huge, dramatic mistakes, but enough that someone noticed. And there was one surveillance cue I missed, which… that’s not like me. I keep replaying it because I know better.\n\nI actually got a formal warning, and that’s probably what made me realize this isn’t just me having a rough few days. I’ve never really had that happen before. I’m usually dependable, I do the job, I keep my head down. So yeah, I’m functioning, technically, but it’s affecting my work now in a way it never used to.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I’ve had bad stretches before, like anyone, where I was down for a few days or stressed out about work or money or whatever was going on at the time. But I don’t know if I’d say it was like this.\n\nI’m trying to think back and not exaggerate either way. There were times where I wasn’t in a great place, but usually I could point to a reason and it would pass, or I’d still feel like myself underneath it. This feels more… I don’t know, more stuck, I guess.\n\nSo I don’t think I’ve had a period exactly like this before, not that I can clearly remember. But I also haven’t really sat down and thought about it in this kind of way before, so I’m not completely sure.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that’s been getting me in trouble at work.

When I’m watching the CCTV feeds, I’ll be staring right at the monitors, but my mind just kind of… slips off somewhere. Not even somewhere important, sometimes it’s just blank or random thoughts, and then I realize I wasn’t really taking in what I was looking at for a few seconds or longer. In that job, even a little lapse like that matters.

And with incident reports, it’s been frustrating because I’ll write something, then reread it, and I still don’t trust that I actually understood what I wrote. I end up going over the same sentence again and again, or checking details I normally would’ve caught the first time. It feels like my brain isn’t holding onto things the way it usually does.

That’s how I ended up making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. It’s not that I don’t care or I’m trying to cut corners. I’m trying, but my focus just isn’t staying where it’s supposed to.

That’s actually been one of the weirder parts, because I’m not someone who usually has a hard time filling my time. I used to really look forward to going for a run in the morning — nothing intense, just getting out before the day started, clearing my head a little. Lately I’ll set my shoes out or think, “Okay, tomorrow I’m going,” and then when the time comes it just feels like this huge chore, like I can’t make myself care enough to do it.\n\nSame with playing guitar. I’ve always kept it nearby, and normally I’d pick it up without even thinking about it, just mess around for a while. Now it kind of just sits there. I look at it and think I should play, but there’s no pull toward it, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd cooking dinner too — I used to actually enjoy that, planning what I was going to make, getting it started after work. Now it’s like every step feels like too much, and not in a busy way, more like… I just don’t get anything out of it. It all feels like another task I’m supposed to force myself through.\n\nDoctor: About how long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’d say it’s been about three weeks now. It kind of started after they reorganized the security operations at the casino and changed around my assignments. At first I thought I was just reacting to the schedule and the different routine, you know, like maybe I just needed a few days to adjust.\n\nBut it didn’t really lift. It’s been pretty steady since then — not every single minute is exactly the same, but overall, yeah, about three weeks of feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this started because of the changes at work?\n\nPatient: I think so, yeah. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like one thing at work magically caused everything, but the timing lines up pretty clearly in my head. It’s been going on about three weeks, and that’s right around when the casino reorganized the security operations and changed my assignments.\n\nAt first I honestly thought I was just thrown off by the change — different responsibilities, different rhythm to the day, having to adjust to how things were being handled. I figured maybe after a few shifts I’d settle in and it would pass. But it didn’t really pass. It just kind of stayed with me. So yeah, I’d say it started after those changes at work, or at least that’s when I noticed it beginning.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d use the word hopeless exactly every time, but most days there’s this heavy, flat feeling that I’m carrying around. Like I can be at home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work, and I’m physically there, I’m answering people, I’m going through the motions, but inside it feels like I’m not really there.\n\nIt’s not always some dramatic thought like “nothing will ever get better.” It’s more like everything feels dulled out and hard to reach, if that makes sense. Like I’m looking at my normal life from a distance and I can’t quite get back into it. That part has been pretty unsettling.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of wanting to die or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I haven’t made any plans to hurt myself or anything like that. I don’t… I don’t want to scare anybody by saying it wrong.\n\nBut yeah, I have had thoughts where I think, like, if I just didn’t wake up in the morning, maybe that wouldn’t be the worst thing. Not that I’m trying to make that happen, and I’m not sitting there figuring out how to do something to myself. It’s more like when I’m really tired of pushing through the day, this thought comes in that maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t have to keep doing this.\n\nIt’s hard to admit that out loud, because I don’t want it to sound like I’m about to do something. I’m not. But the thought has been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight since this started?\n\nPatient: I don’t know exactly how many pounds, because I haven’t really been checking the scale every day or anything, but I wouldn’t be surprised if I have. The bigger thing I’ve noticed is my appetite’s been really off these last few weeks.\n\nThat’s actually one of the reasons I came in. I’ll get home after a shift and there’ll be food I normally like, stuff I’d usually have no problem eating, and it just kind of sits there in front of me. I’ll take a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it feels like I’m forcing it down more than actually wanting it. It doesn’t seem appealing at all.\n\nI’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it feels forced, like I’m chewing just to get it over with. And then I end up leaving most of it there. It’s not really like me, so after it kept happening I figured I should probably talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I don’t know if I would’ve said “depressed” right away, because I keep trying to tell myself I’m just tired or in a funk or whatever, but most days there’s this heavy, flat feeling that kind of follows me around.\n\nIt’s not like I’m crying all the time or anything dramatic like that. It’s more like… even when I’m home with my family, or I’m sitting in the break room at work and people are talking, I’m there physically, but I don’t really feel there. Like I’m just sort of watching everything from behind glass. I can answer people and do what I’m supposed to do, but inside it feels dull and weighed down. It’s been hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting less enjoyment than usual from things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. That’s actually been one of the weirder parts, because I’m not someone who usually has a hard time filling my time. I used to really look forward to going for a run in the morning — nothing intense, just getting out before the day started, clearing my head a little. Lately I’ll set my shoes out or think, “Okay, tomorrow I’m going,” and then when the time comes it just feels like this huge chore, like I can’t make myself care enough to do it.\n\nSame with playing guitar. I’ve always kept it nearby, and normally I’d pick it up without even thinking about it, just mess around for a while. Now it kind of just sits there. I look at it and think I should play, but there’s no pull toward it, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd cooking dinner too — I used to actually enjoy that, planning what I was going to make, getting it started after work. Now it’s like every step feels like too much, and not in a busy way, more like… I just don’t get anything out of it. It all feels like another task I’m supposed to force myself through.\n\nDoctor: About how long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’d say it’s been about three weeks now. It kind of started after they reorganized the security operations at the casino and changed around my assignments. At first I thought I was just reacting to the schedule and the different routine, you know, like maybe I just needed a few days to adjust.\n\nBut it didn’t really lift. It’s been pretty steady since then — not every single minute is exactly the same, but overall, yeah, about three weeks of feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this started because of the changes at work?\n\nPatient: I think so, yeah. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like one thing at work magically caused everything, but the timing lines up pretty clearly in my head. It’s been going on about three weeks, and that’s right around when the casino reorganized the security operations and changed my assignments.\n\nAt first I honestly thought I was just thrown off by the change — different responsibilities, different rhythm to the day, having to adjust to how things were being handled. I figured maybe after a few shifts I’d settle in and it would pass. But it didn’t really pass. It just kind of stayed with me. So yeah, I’d say it started after those changes at work, or at least that’s when I noticed it beginning.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d use the word hopeless exactly every time, but most days there’s this heavy, flat feeling that I’m carrying around. Like I can be at home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work, and I’m physically there, I’m answering people, I’m going through the motions, but inside it feels like I’m not really there.\n\nIt’s not always some dramatic thought like “nothing will ever get better.” It’s more like everything feels dulled out and hard to reach, if that makes sense. Like I’m looking at my normal life from a distance and I can’t quite get back into it. That part has been pretty unsettling.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of wanting to die or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I haven’t made any plans to hurt myself or anything like that. I don’t… I don’t want to scare anybody by saying it wrong.\n\nBut yeah, I have had thoughts where I think, like, if I just didn’t wake up in the morning, maybe that wouldn’t be the worst thing. Not that I’m trying to make that happen, and I’m not sitting there figuring out how to do something to myself. It’s more like when I’m really tired of pushing through the day, this thought comes in that maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t have to keep doing this.\n\nIt’s hard to admit that out loud, because I don’t want it to sound like I’m about to do something. I’m not. But the thought has been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight since this started?\n\nPatient: I don’t know exactly how many pounds, because I haven’t really been checking the scale every day or anything, but I wouldn’t be surprised if I have. The bigger thing I’ve noticed is my appetite’s been really off these last few weeks.\n\nThat’s actually one of the reasons I came in. I’ll get home after a shift and there’ll be food I normally like, stuff I’d usually have no problem eating, and it just kind of sits there in front of me. I’ll take a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it feels like I’m forcing it down more than actually wanting it. So maybe I’ve lost some weight, yeah, but mostly I’ve just barely been eating like I normally do.\n\nDoctor: How have you been sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that in a neat way. I haven’t been keeping track of it like, “I slept this many hours,” or anything. Some nights I’m in bed and it feels like I’ve been lying there a long time, but then I don’t know if I actually slept more than I think I did or less than I think I did.\n\nI guess what I can say is it hasn’t felt very restful. I’ll go to bed because I know I need to, especially with work and everything, but when morning comes I don’t always feel like I got much out of it. But I can’t say exactly how many hours, or whether it’s every single night. It’s just been… off, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still going in. I haven’t stopped showing up for work or anything, and at home I’m still doing the basic things I have to do, even if it takes me longer or I kind of drag myself through it.\n\nBut at work, it’s starting to show in ways that really bother me. My job is the kind where you’re supposed to be paying attention, writing things down correctly, catching things when they happen. And I’ve made some documentation mistakes lately that I normally wouldn’t make. Not huge, dramatic mistakes, but enough that someone noticed. And there was one surveillance cue I missed, which… that’s not like me. I keep replaying it because I know better.\n\nI actually got a formal warning, and that’s probably what made me realize this isn’t just me having a rough few days. I’ve never really had that happen before. I’m usually dependable, I do the job, I keep my head down. So yeah, I’m functioning, technically, but it’s affecting my work now in a way it never used to.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I’ve had bad stretches before, like anyone, where I was down for a few days or stressed out about work or money or whatever was going on at the time. But I don’t know if I’d say it was like this.\n\nI’m trying to think back and not exaggerate either way. There were times where I wasn’t in a great place, but usually I could point to a reason and it would pass, or I’d still feel like myself underneath it. This feels more… I don’t know, more stuck, I guess.\n\nSo I don’t think I’ve had a period exactly like this before, not that I can clearly remember. But I also haven’t really sat down and thought about it in this kind of way before, so I’m not completely sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that’s been getting me in trouble at work.\n\nWhen I’m watching the CCTV feeds, I’ll be staring right at the monitors, but my mind just kind of… slips off somewhere. Not even somewhere important, sometimes it’s just blank or random thoughts, and then I realize I wasn’t really taking in what I was looking at for a few seconds or longer. In that job, even a little lapse like that matters.\n\nAnd with incident reports, it’s been frustrating because I’ll write something, then reread it, and I still don’t trust that I actually understood what I wrote. I end up going over the same sentence again and again, or checking details I normally would’ve caught the first time. It feels like my brain isn’t holding onto things the way it usually does.\n\nThat’s how I ended up making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. It’s not that I don’t care or I’m trying to cut corners. I’m trying, but my focus just isn’t staying where it’s supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually energetic or high, needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing.\n\nI mean, I’ve had times where I stayed up late or had a busy stretch and maybe got less sleep because I had things to do, but I wouldn’t say I felt unusually high or full of energy from it. If anything, when I don’t sleep enough I usually feel it. I get tired, slower, kind of run down.\n\nI’m trying to think if there was ever a time where people would’ve said I was acting different, like really wired or more energetic than usual, and nothing really comes to mind. Not like days where I barely needed sleep and still felt great or anything like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… I think it is stronger than just work stuff. I mean, the changes at work definitely didn’t help, and I can point to certain things there that make me feel worse, but this feels bigger than that.

I don’t… I don’t want to scare anybody by saying it wrong.\n\nBut yeah, I have had thoughts where I think, like, if I just didn’t wake up in the morning, maybe that wouldn’t be the worst thing. It’s more like when I’m really tired of pushing through the day, this thought comes in that maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t have to keep doing this.\n\nIt’s hard to admit that out loud, because I don’t want it to sound like I’m about to do something. I’m not. But the thought has been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight since this started?\n\nPatient: I don’t know exactly how many pounds, because I haven’t really been checking the scale every day or anything, but I wouldn’t be surprised if I have. The bigger thing I’ve noticed is my appetite’s been really off these last few weeks.\n\nThat’s actually one of the reasons I came in. I’ll get home after a shift and there’ll be food I normally like, stuff I’d usually have no problem eating, and it just kind of sits there in front of me. I’ll take a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it feels like I’m forcing it down more than actually wanting it. So maybe I’ve lost some weight, yeah, but mostly I’ve just barely been eating like I normally do.\n\nDoctor: How have you been sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that in a neat way. I haven’t been keeping track of it like, “I slept this many hours,” or anything. Some nights I’m in bed and it feels like I’ve been lying there a long time, but then I don’t know if I actually slept more than I think I did or less than I think I did.\n\nI guess what I can say is it hasn’t felt very restful. I’ll go to bed because I know I need to, especially with work and everything, but when morning comes I don’t always feel like I got much out of it. But I can’t say exactly how many hours, or whether it’s every single night. It’s just been… off, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still going in. I haven’t stopped showing up for work or anything, and at home I’m still doing the basic things I have to do, even if it takes me longer or I kind of drag myself through it.\n\nBut at work, it’s starting to show in ways that really bother me. My job is the kind where you’re supposed to be paying attention, writing things down correctly, catching things when they happen. And I’ve made some documentation mistakes lately that I normally wouldn’t make. Not huge, dramatic mistakes, but enough that someone noticed. And there was one surveillance cue I missed, which… that’s not like me. I keep replaying it because I know better.\n\nI actually got a formal warning, and that’s probably what made me realize this isn’t just me having a rough few days. I’ve never really had that happen before. I’m usually dependable, I do the job, I keep my head down. So yeah, I’m functioning, technically, but it’s affecting my work now in a way it never used to.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I’ve had bad stretches before, like anyone, where I was down for a few days or stressed out about work or money or whatever was going on at the time. But I don’t know if I’d say it was like this.\n\nI’m trying to think back and not exaggerate either way. There were times where I wasn’t in a great place, but usually I could point to a reason and it would pass, or I’d still feel like myself underneath it. This feels more… I don’t know, more stuck, I guess.\n\nSo I don’t think I’ve had a period exactly like this before, not that I can clearly remember. But I also haven’t really sat down and thought about it in this kind of way before, so I’m not completely sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that’s been getting me in trouble at work.\n\nWhen I’m watching the CCTV feeds, I’ll be staring right at the monitors, but my mind just kind of… slips off somewhere. Not even somewhere important, sometimes it’s just blank or random thoughts, and then I realize I wasn’t really taking in what I was looking at for a few seconds or longer. In that job, even a little lapse like that matters.\n\nAnd with incident reports, it’s been frustrating because I’ll write something, then reread it, and I still don’t trust that I actually understood what I wrote. I end up going over the same sentence again and again, or checking details I normally would’ve caught the first time. It feels like my brain isn’t holding onto things the way it usually does.\n\nThat’s how I ended up making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. It’s not that I don’t care or I’m trying to cut corners. I’m trying, but my focus just isn’t staying where it’s supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually energetic or high, needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing.\n\nI mean, I’ve had times where I stayed up late or had a busy stretch and maybe got less sleep because I had things to do, but I wouldn’t say I felt unusually high or full of energy from it. If anything, when I don’t sleep enough I usually feel it. I get tired, slower, kind of run down.\n\nI’m trying to think if there was ever a time where people would’ve said I was acting different, like really wired or more energetic than usual, and nothing really comes to mind. Not like days where I barely needed sleep and still felt great or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than you would expect just from the changes at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it is stronger than just work stuff. I mean, the changes at work definitely didn’t help, and I can point to certain things there that make me feel worse, but this feels bigger than that.\n\nMost days it’s like I’m carrying this heavy, flat feeling around with me. It’s not just when I’m on shift or dealing with something stressful. Even when I’m home with my family, or just sitting in the break room, or doing nothing in particular, I still feel kind of… not fully there. Like everything is happening around me and I’m present physically, but inside I’m just dulled out.\n\nThat’s what makes me think it’s not only about the job changes. If it were just work, I’d expect maybe I’d feel better once I got home or once I was away from it for a while. But a lot of the time the feeling comes with me, and it just sits there.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Like everything is happening around me and I’m present physically, but inside I’m just dulled out.\n\nThat’s what makes me think it’s not only about the job changes. If it were just work, I’d expect maybe I’d feel better once I got home or once I was away from it for a while. But a lot of the time the feeling comes with me, and it just sits there.\n\n[Last Question]\nCompared with what changed at work, does your depression feel out of proportion to the actual work stress?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 39 / security_guard_or_gambling_surveillance_officer / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Depressed_Mood\nmanifestation: \"Most days I feel like I’m carrying this heavy, flat feeling around, like even when I’m home with my family or sitting in the break room, I’m just not really there.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… it does. I know work has been stressful, and I don’t want to act like that part is nothing, because it does get to me. But this feels heavier than what I’d expect from just schedule changes or pressure at work.

It’s more like most days I’m carrying this heavy, flat feeling around with me. And it doesn’t really shut off when I leave. I can be home with my family, or just sitting in the break room, and I’m still kind of… not really there. Like I’m there physically, but inside I feel dulled out, like everything is muted.

So yeah, compared to the actual work stress, it feels out of proportion. It follows me around more than I think normal job stress should.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.9, F43.2, F33.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess the main thing that got me to finally come in is my appetite has been really off. Like, for the last few weeks I’ll get home after a shift and normally there are certain foods I look forward to, you know, stuff I usually have no problem eating, but lately it’s like I just stare at it. It doesn’t seem appealing at all.\n\nI’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it feels forced, like I’m chewing just to get it over with. And then I end up leaving most of it there. It’s not really like me, so after it kept happening I figured I should probably talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I don’t know if I would’ve said “depressed” right away, because I keep trying to tell myself I’m just tired or in a funk or whatever, but most days there’s this heavy, flat feeling that kind of follows me around.\n\nIt’s not like I’m crying all the time or anything dramatic like that. It’s more like… even when I’m home with my family, or I’m sitting in the break room at work and people are talking, I’m there physically, but I don’t really feel there. Like I’m just sort of watching everything from behind glass. I can answer people and do what I’m supposed to do, but inside it feels dull and weighed down. It’s been hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting less enjoyment than usual from things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. That’s actually been one of the weirder parts, because I’m not someone who usually has a hard time filling my time. I used to really look forward to going for a run in the morning — nothing intense, just getting out before the day started, clearing my head a little. Lately I’ll set my shoes out or think, “Okay, tomorrow I’m going,” and then when the time comes it just feels like this huge chore, like I can’t make myself care enough to do it.\n\nSame with playing guitar. I’ve always kept it nearby, and normally I’d pick it up without even thinking about it, just mess around for a while. Now it kind of just sits there. I look at it and think I should play, but there’s no pull toward it, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd cooking dinner too — I used to actually enjoy that, planning what I was going to make, getting it started after work. Now it’s like every step feels like too much, and not in a busy way, more like… I just don’t get anything out of it. It all feels like another task I’m supposed to force myself through.\n\nDoctor: About how long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’d say it’s been about three weeks now. It kind of started after they reorganized the security operations at the casino and changed around my assignments. At first I thought I was just reacting to the schedule and the different routine, you know, like maybe I just needed a few days to adjust.\n\nBut it didn’t really lift. It’s been pretty steady since then — not every single minute is exactly the same, but overall, yeah, about three weeks of feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this started because of the changes at work?\n\nPatient: I think so, yeah. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like one thing at work magically caused everything, but the timing lines up pretty clearly in my head. It’s been going on about three weeks, and that’s right around when the casino reorganized the security operations and changed my assignments.\n\nAt first I honestly thought I was just thrown off by the change — different responsibilities, different rhythm to the day, having to adjust to how things were being handled. I figured maybe after a few shifts I’d settle in and it would pass. But it didn’t really pass. It just kind of stayed with me. So yeah, I’d say it started after those changes at work, or at least that’s when I noticed it beginning.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d use the word hopeless exactly every time, but most days there’s this heavy, flat feeling that I’m carrying around. Like I can be at home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work, and I’m physically there, I’m answering people, I’m going through the motions, but inside it feels like I’m not really there.\n\nIt’s not always some dramatic thought like “nothing will ever get better.” It’s more like everything feels dulled out and hard to reach, if that makes sense. Like I’m looking at my normal life from a distance and I can’t quite get back into it. That part has been pretty unsettling.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of wanting to die or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I haven’t made any plans to hurt myself or anything like that. I don’t… I don’t want to scare anybody by saying it wrong.\n\nBut yeah, I have had thoughts where I think, like, if I just didn’t wake up in the morning, maybe that wouldn’t be the worst thing. Not that I’m trying to make that happen, and I’m not sitting there figuring out how to do something to myself. It’s more like when I’m really tired of pushing through the day, this thought comes in that maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t have to keep doing this.\n\nIt’s hard to admit that out loud, because I don’t want it to sound like I’m about to do something. I’m not. But the thought has been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight since this started?\n\nPatient: I don’t know exactly how many pounds, because I haven’t really been checking the scale every day or anything, but I wouldn’t be surprised if I have. The bigger thing I’ve noticed is my appetite’s been really off these last few weeks.\n\nThat’s actually one of the reasons I came in. I’ll get home after a shift and there’ll be food I normally like, stuff I’d usually have no problem eating, and it just kind of sits there in front of me. I’ll take a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it feels like I’m forcing it down more than actually wanting it. So maybe I’ve lost some weight, yeah, but mostly I’ve just barely been eating like I normally do.\n\nDoctor: How have you been sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that in a neat way. I haven’t been keeping track of it like, “I slept this many hours,” or anything. Some nights I’m in bed and it feels like I’ve been lying there a long time, but then I don’t know if I actually slept more than I think I did or less than I think I did.\n\nI guess what I can say is it hasn’t felt very restful. I’ll go to bed because I know I need to, especially with work and everything, but when morning comes I don’t always feel like I got much out of it. But I can’t say exactly how many hours, or whether it’s every single night. It’s just been… off, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still going in. I haven’t stopped showing up for work or anything, and at home I’m still doing the basic things I have to do, even if it takes me longer or I kind of drag myself through it.\n\nBut at work, it’s starting to show in ways that really bother me. My job is the kind where you’re supposed to be paying attention, writing things down correctly, catching things when they happen. And I’ve made some documentation mistakes lately that I normally wouldn’t make. Not huge, dramatic mistakes, but enough that someone noticed. And there was one surveillance cue I missed, which… that’s not like me. I keep replaying it because I know better.\n\nI actually got a formal warning, and that’s probably what made me realize this isn’t just me having a rough few days. I’ve never really had that happen before. I’m usually dependable, I do the job, I keep my head down. So yeah, I’m functioning, technically, but it’s affecting my work now in a way it never used to.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I’ve had bad stretches before, like anyone, where I was down for a few days or stressed out about work or money or whatever was going on at the time. But I don’t know if I’d say it was like this.\n\nI’m trying to think back and not exaggerate either way. There were times where I wasn’t in a great place, but usually I could point to a reason and it would pass, or I’d still feel like myself underneath it. This feels more… I don’t know, more stuck, I guess.\n\nSo I don’t think I’ve had a period exactly like this before, not that I can clearly remember. But I also haven’t really sat down and thought about it in this kind of way before, so I’m not completely sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that’s been getting me in trouble at work.\n\nWhen I’m watching the CCTV feeds, I’ll be staring right at the monitors, but my mind just kind of… slips off somewhere. Not even somewhere important, sometimes it’s just blank or random thoughts, and then I realize I wasn’t really taking in what I was looking at for a few seconds or longer. In that job, even a little lapse like that matters.\n\nAnd with incident reports, it’s been frustrating because I’ll write something, then reread it, and I still don’t trust that I actually understood what I wrote. I end up going over the same sentence again and again, or checking details I normally would’ve caught the first time. It feels like my brain isn’t holding onto things the way it usually does.\n\nThat’s how I ended up making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. It’s not that I don’t care or I’m trying to cut corners. I’m trying, but my focus just isn’t staying where it’s supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually energetic or high, needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing.\n\nI mean, I’ve had times where I stayed up late or had a busy stretch and maybe got less sleep because I had things to do, but I wouldn’t say I felt unusually high or full of energy from it. If anything, when I don’t sleep enough I usually feel it. I get tired, slower, kind of run down.\n\nI’m trying to think if there was ever a time where people would’ve said I was acting different, like really wired or more energetic than usual, and nothing really comes to mind. Not like days where I barely needed sleep and still felt great or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than you would expect just from the changes at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it is stronger than just work stuff. I mean, the changes at work definitely didn’t help, and I can point to certain things there that make me feel worse, but this feels bigger than that.\n\nMost days it’s like I’m carrying this heavy, flat feeling around with me. It’s not just when I’m on shift or dealing with something stressful. Even when I’m home with my family, or just sitting in the break room, or doing nothing in particular, I still feel kind of… not fully there. Like everything is happening around me and I’m present physically, but inside I’m just dulled out.\n\nThat’s what makes me think it’s not only about the job changes. If it were just work, I’d expect maybe I’d feel better once I got home or once I was away from it for a while. But a lot of the time the feeling comes with me, and it just sits there.\n\nDoctor: Compared with what changed at work, does your depression feel out of proportion to the actual work stress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it does. I know work has been stressful, and I don’t want to act like that part is nothing, because it does get to me. But this feels heavier than what I’d expect from just schedule changes or pressure at work.\n\nIt’s more like most days I’m carrying this heavy, flat feeling around with me. And it doesn’t really shut off when I leave. I can be home with my family, or just sitting in the break room, and I’m still kind of… not really there. Like I’m there physically, but inside I feel dulled out, like everything is muted.\n\nSo yeah, compared to the actual work stress, it feels out of proportion. It follows me around more than I think normal job stress should.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

It’s been hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting less enjoyment than usual from things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. That’s actually been one of the weirder parts, because I’m not someone who usually has a hard time filling my time. I used to really look forward to going for a run in the morning — nothing intense, just getting out before the day started, clearing my head a little. Lately I’ll set my shoes out or think, “Okay, tomorrow I’m going,” and then when the time comes it just feels like this huge chore, like I can’t make myself care enough to do it.\n\nSame with playing guitar. I’ve always kept it nearby, and normally I’d pick it up without even thinking about it, just mess around for a while. Now it kind of just sits there. I look at it and think I should play, but there’s no pull toward it, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd cooking dinner too — I used to actually enjoy that, planning what I was going to make, getting it started after work. Now it’s like every step feels like too much, and not in a busy way, more like… I just don’t get anything out of it. It all feels like another task I’m supposed to force myself through.\n\nDoctor: About how long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’d say it’s been about three weeks now. It kind of started after they reorganized the security operations at the casino and changed around my assignments. At first I thought I was just reacting to the schedule and the different routine, you know, like maybe I just needed a few days to adjust.\n\nBut it didn’t really lift. It’s been pretty steady since then — not every single minute is exactly the same, but overall, yeah, about three weeks of feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this started because of the changes at work?\n\nPatient: I think so, yeah. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like one thing at work magically caused everything, but the timing lines up pretty clearly in my head. It’s been going on about three weeks, and that’s right around when the casino reorganized the security operations and changed my assignments.\n\nAt first I honestly thought I was just thrown off by the change — different responsibilities, different rhythm to the day, having to adjust to how things were being handled. I figured maybe after a few shifts I’d settle in and it would pass. But it didn’t really pass. It just kind of stayed with me. So yeah, I’d say it started after those changes at work, or at least that’s when I noticed it beginning.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d use the word hopeless exactly every time, but most days there’s this heavy, flat feeling that I’m carrying around. Like I can be at home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work, and I’m physically there, I’m answering people, I’m going through the motions, but inside it feels like I’m not really there.\n\nIt’s not always some dramatic thought like “nothing will ever get better.” It’s more like everything feels dulled out and hard to reach, if that makes sense. Like I’m looking at my normal life from a distance and I can’t quite get back into it. That part has been pretty unsettling.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of wanting to die or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I haven’t made any plans to hurt myself or anything like that. I don’t… I don’t want to scare anybody by saying it wrong.\n\nBut yeah, I have had thoughts where I think, like, if I just didn’t wake up in the morning, maybe that wouldn’t be the worst thing. Not that I’m trying to make that happen, and I’m not sitting there figuring out how to do something to myself. It’s more like when I’m really tired of pushing through the day, this thought comes in that maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t have to keep doing this.\n\nIt’s hard to admit that out loud, because I don’t want it to sound like I’m about to do something. I’m not. But the thought has been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight since this started?\n\nPatient: I don’t know exactly how many pounds, because I haven’t really been checking the scale every day or anything, but I wouldn’t be surprised if I have. The bigger thing I’ve noticed is my appetite’s been really off these last few weeks.\n\nThat’s actually one of the reasons I came in. I’ll get home after a shift and there’ll be food I normally like, stuff I’d usually have no problem eating, and it just kind of sits there in front of me. I’ll take a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it feels like I’m forcing it down more than actually wanting it. So maybe I’ve lost some weight, yeah, but mostly I’ve just barely been eating like I normally do.\n\nDoctor: How have you been sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that in a neat way. I haven’t been keeping track of it like, “I slept this many hours,” or anything. Some nights I’m in bed and it feels like I’ve been lying there a long time, but then I don’t know if I actually slept more than I think I did or less than I think I did.\n\nI guess what I can say is it hasn’t felt very restful. I’ll go to bed because I know I need to, especially with work and everything, but when morning comes I don’t always feel like I got much out of it. But I can’t say exactly how many hours, or whether it’s every single night. It’s just been… off, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still going in. I haven’t stopped showing up for work or anything, and at home I’m still doing the basic things I have to do, even if it takes me longer or I kind of drag myself through it.\n\nBut at work, it’s starting to show in ways that really bother me. My job is the kind where you’re supposed to be paying attention, writing things down correctly, catching things when they happen. And I’ve made some documentation mistakes lately that I normally wouldn’t make. Not huge, dramatic mistakes, but enough that someone noticed. And there was one surveillance cue I missed, which… that’s not like me. I keep replaying it because I know better.\n\nI actually got a formal warning, and that’s probably what made me realize this isn’t just me having a rough few days. I’ve never really had that happen before. I’m usually dependable, I do the job, I keep my head down. So yeah, I’m functioning, technically, but it’s affecting my work now in a way it never used to.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I’ve had bad stretches before, like anyone, where I was down for a few days or stressed out about work or money or whatever was going on at the time. But I don’t know if I’d say it was like this.\n\nI’m trying to think back and not exaggerate either way. There were times where I wasn’t in a great place, but usually I could point to a reason and it would pass, or I’d still feel like myself underneath it. This feels more… I don’t know, more stuck, I guess.\n\nSo I don’t think I’ve had a period exactly like this before, not that I can clearly remember. But I also haven’t really sat down and thought about it in this kind of way before, so I’m not completely sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that’s been getting me in trouble at work.\n\nWhen I’m watching the CCTV feeds, I’ll be staring right at the monitors, but my mind just kind of… slips off somewhere. Not even somewhere important, sometimes it’s just blank or random thoughts, and then I realize I wasn’t really taking in what I was looking at for a few seconds or longer. In that job, even a little lapse like that matters.\n\nAnd with incident reports, it’s been frustrating because I’ll write something, then reread it, and I still don’t trust that I actually understood what I wrote. I end up going over the same sentence again and again, or checking details I normally would’ve caught the first time. It feels like my brain isn’t holding onto things the way it usually does.\n\nThat’s how I ended up making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. It’s not that I don’t care or I’m trying to cut corners. I’m trying, but my focus just isn’t staying where it’s supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually energetic or high, needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing.\n\nI mean, I’ve had times where I stayed up late or had a busy stretch and maybe got less sleep because I had things to do, but I wouldn’t say I felt unusually high or full of energy from it. If anything, when I don’t sleep enough I usually feel it. I get tired, slower, kind of run down.\n\nI’m trying to think if there was ever a time where people would’ve said I was acting different, like really wired or more energetic than usual, and nothing really comes to mind. Not like days where I barely needed sleep and still felt great or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than you would expect just from the changes at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it is stronger than just work stuff. I mean, the changes at work definitely didn’t help, and I can point to certain things there that make me feel worse, but this feels bigger than that.\n\nMost days it’s like I’m carrying this heavy, flat feeling around with me. It’s not just when I’m on shift or dealing with something stressful. Even when I’m home with my family, or just sitting in the break room, or doing nothing in particular, I still feel kind of… not fully there. Like everything is happening around me and I’m present physically, but inside I’m just dulled out.\n\nThat’s what makes me think it’s not only about the job changes. If it were just work, I’d expect maybe I’d feel better once I got home or once I was away from it for a while. But a lot of the time the feeling comes with me, and it just sits there.\n\nDoctor: Compared with what changed at work, does your depression feel out of proportion to the actual work stress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it does. I know work has been stressful, and I don’t want to act like that part is nothing, because it does get to me. But this feels heavier than what I’d expect from just schedule changes or pressure at work.\n\nIt’s more like most days I’m carrying this heavy, flat feeling around with me. Like I’m there physically, but inside I feel dulled out, like everything is muted.\n\nSo yeah, compared to the actual work stress, it feels out of proportion. It follows me around more than I think normal job stress should.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. There were probably times I wasn’t myself, maybe quieter or more discouraged, but I didn’t really keep track of it, you know? I wasn’t marking days on a calendar or thinking, “This has been going on for two weeks.”\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I don’t know for sure. I figured maybe after a few shifts I’d settle in and it would pass. But it didn’t really pass. It just kind of stayed with me. So yeah, I’d say it started after those changes at work, or at least that’s when I noticed it beginning.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d use the word hopeless exactly every time, but most days there’s this heavy, flat feeling that I’m carrying around. Like I can be at home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work, and I’m physically there, I’m answering people, I’m going through the motions, but inside it feels like I’m not really there.\n\nIt’s not always some dramatic thought like “nothing will ever get better.” It’s more like everything feels dulled out and hard to reach, if that makes sense. Like I’m looking at my normal life from a distance and I can’t quite get back into it. That part has been pretty unsettling.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of wanting to die or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I haven’t made any plans to hurt myself or anything like that. I don’t… I don’t want to scare anybody by saying it wrong.\n\nBut yeah, I have had thoughts where I think, like, if I just didn’t wake up in the morning, maybe that wouldn’t be the worst thing. Not that I’m trying to make that happen, and I’m not sitting there figuring out how to do something to myself. It’s more like when I’m really tired of pushing through the day, this thought comes in that maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t have to keep doing this.\n\nIt’s hard to admit that out loud, because I don’t want it to sound like I’m about to do something. I’m not. But the thought has been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight since this started?\n\nPatient: I don’t know exactly how many pounds, because I haven’t really been checking the scale every day or anything, but I wouldn’t be surprised if I have. The bigger thing I’ve noticed is my appetite’s been really off these last few weeks.\n\nThat’s actually one of the reasons I came in. I’ll get home after a shift and there’ll be food I normally like, stuff I’d usually have no problem eating, and it just kind of sits there in front of me. I’ll take a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it feels like I’m forcing it down more than actually wanting it. So maybe I’ve lost some weight, yeah, but mostly I’ve just barely been eating like I normally do.\n\nDoctor: How have you been sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that in a neat way. I haven’t been keeping track of it like, “I slept this many hours,” or anything. Some nights I’m in bed and it feels like I’ve been lying there a long time, but then I don’t know if I actually slept more than I think I did or less than I think I did.\n\nI guess what I can say is it hasn’t felt very restful. I’ll go to bed because I know I need to, especially with work and everything, but when morning comes I don’t always feel like I got much out of it. But I can’t say exactly how many hours, or whether it’s every single night. It’s just been… off, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still going in. I haven’t stopped showing up for work or anything, and at home I’m still doing the basic things I have to do, even if it takes me longer or I kind of drag myself through it.\n\nBut at work, it’s starting to show in ways that really bother me. My job is the kind where you’re supposed to be paying attention, writing things down correctly, catching things when they happen. And I’ve made some documentation mistakes lately that I normally wouldn’t make. Not huge, dramatic mistakes, but enough that someone noticed. And there was one surveillance cue I missed, which… that’s not like me. I keep replaying it because I know better.\n\nI actually got a formal warning, and that’s probably what made me realize this isn’t just me having a rough few days. I’ve never really had that happen before. I’m usually dependable, I do the job, I keep my head down. So yeah, I’m functioning, technically, but it’s affecting my work now in a way it never used to.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I’ve had bad stretches before, like anyone, where I was down for a few days or stressed out about work or money or whatever was going on at the time. But I don’t know if I’d say it was like this.\n\nI’m trying to think back and not exaggerate either way. There were times where I wasn’t in a great place, but usually I could point to a reason and it would pass, or I’d still feel like myself underneath it. This feels more… I don’t know, more stuck, I guess.\n\nSo I don’t think I’ve had a period exactly like this before, not that I can clearly remember. But I also haven’t really sat down and thought about it in this kind of way before, so I’m not completely sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that’s been getting me in trouble at work.\n\nWhen I’m watching the CCTV feeds, I’ll be staring right at the monitors, but my mind just kind of… slips off somewhere. Not even somewhere important, sometimes it’s just blank or random thoughts, and then I realize I wasn’t really taking in what I was looking at for a few seconds or longer. In that job, even a little lapse like that matters.\n\nAnd with incident reports, it’s been frustrating because I’ll write something, then reread it, and I still don’t trust that I actually understood what I wrote. I end up going over the same sentence again and again, or checking details I normally would’ve caught the first time. It feels like my brain isn’t holding onto things the way it usually does.\n\nThat’s how I ended up making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. It’s not that I don’t care or I’m trying to cut corners. I’m trying, but my focus just isn’t staying where it’s supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually energetic or high, needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing.\n\nI mean, I’ve had times where I stayed up late or had a busy stretch and maybe got less sleep because I had things to do, but I wouldn’t say I felt unusually high or full of energy from it. If anything, when I don’t sleep enough I usually feel it. I get tired, slower, kind of run down.\n\nI’m trying to think if there was ever a time where people would’ve said I was acting different, like really wired or more energetic than usual, and nothing really comes to mind. Not like days where I barely needed sleep and still felt great or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than you would expect just from the changes at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it is stronger than just work stuff. I mean, the changes at work definitely didn’t help, and I can point to certain things there that make me feel worse, but this feels bigger than that.\n\nMost days it’s like I’m carrying this heavy, flat feeling around with me. It’s not just when I’m on shift or dealing with something stressful. Even when I’m home with my family, or just sitting in the break room, or doing nothing in particular, I still feel kind of… not fully there. Like everything is happening around me and I’m present physically, but inside I’m just dulled out.\n\nThat’s what makes me think it’s not only about the job changes. If it were just work, I’d expect maybe I’d feel better once I got home or once I was away from it for a while. But a lot of the time the feeling comes with me, and it just sits there.\n\nDoctor: Compared with what changed at work, does your depression feel out of proportion to the actual work stress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it does. I know work has been stressful, and I don’t want to act like that part is nothing, because it does get to me. But this feels heavier than what I’d expect from just schedule changes or pressure at work.\n\nIt’s more like most days I’m carrying this heavy, flat feeling around with me. And it doesn’t really shut off when I leave. I can be home with my family, or just sitting in the break room, and I’m still kind of… not really there. Like I’m there physically, but inside I feel dulled out, like everything is muted.\n\nSo yeah, compared to the actual work stress, it feels out of proportion. It follows me around more than I think normal job stress should.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, like anyone, where I felt down or stressed for a while, but I don’t know if I’d say it was like this or that it clearly lasted two weeks or more.\n\nI’m trying to think back, and nothing stands out in the same way this current period does. There were probably times I wasn’t myself, maybe quieter or more discouraged, but I didn’t really keep track of it, you know? I wasn’t marking days on a calendar or thinking, “This has been going on for two weeks.”\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I don’t know for sure. I can’t point to another definite time that was like this.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, was there ever a time in your life when you felt depressed or lost interest in things nearly every day for two weeks or longer?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I’ve been trying to think about that, and I can remember times where I was down or stressed or kind of not myself, but I can’t really say there was a clear period before this where it was nearly every day for two weeks or longer.\n\nLike, maybe there were stretches where I was quieter or less interested in doing things, but I didn’t really think of it that way at the time. I wasn’t paying attention to how many days it lasted or whether it was every day. So I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nNothing before this really stands out to me as being clearly like what’s going on now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Not really… at least nothing that I can point to and say, “That’s what set this off.”

I figured maybe after a few shifts I’d settle in and it would pass. But it didn’t really pass. It just kind of stayed with me. Like I can be at home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work, and I’m physically there, I’m answering people, I’m going through the motions, but inside it feels like I’m not really there.\n\nIt’s not always some dramatic thought like “nothing will ever get better.” It’s more like everything feels dulled out and hard to reach, if that makes sense. Like I’m looking at my normal life from a distance and I can’t quite get back into it. I don’t… I don’t want to scare anybody by saying it wrong.\n\nBut yeah, I have had thoughts where I think, like, if I just didn’t wake up in the morning, maybe that wouldn’t be the worst thing. Not that I’m trying to make that happen, and I’m not sitting there figuring out how to do something to myself. It’s more like when I’m really tired of pushing through the day, this thought comes in that maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t have to keep doing this.\n\nIt’s hard to admit that out loud, because I don’t want it to sound like I’m about to do something. I’m not. But the thought has been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight since this started?\n\nPatient: I don’t know exactly how many pounds, because I haven’t really been checking the scale every day or anything, but I wouldn’t be surprised if I have. I’ll get home after a shift and there’ll be food I normally like, stuff I’d usually have no problem eating, and it just kind of sits there in front of me. I’ll take a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it feels like I’m forcing it down more than actually wanting it. So maybe I’ve lost some weight, yeah, but mostly I’ve just barely been eating like I normally do.\n\nDoctor: How have you been sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that in a neat way. I haven’t been keeping track of it like, “I slept this many hours,” or anything. Some nights I’m in bed and it feels like I’ve been lying there a long time, but then I don’t know if I actually slept more than I think I did or less than I think I did.\n\nI guess what I can say is it hasn’t felt very restful. I’ll go to bed because I know I need to, especially with work and everything, but when morning comes I don’t always feel like I got much out of it. But I can’t say exactly how many hours, or whether it’s every single night. It’s just been… off, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still going in. I haven’t stopped showing up for work or anything, and at home I’m still doing the basic things I have to do, even if it takes me longer or I kind of drag myself through it.\n\nBut at work, it’s starting to show in ways that really bother me. My job is the kind where you’re supposed to be paying attention, writing things down correctly, catching things when they happen. And there was one surveillance cue I missed, which… that’s not like me. I keep replaying it because I know better.\n\nI actually got a formal warning, and that’s probably what made me realize this isn’t just me having a rough few days. I’ve never really had that happen before. I’m usually dependable, I do the job, I keep my head down. I mean, I’ve had bad stretches before, like anyone, where I was down for a few days or stressed out about work or money or whatever was going on at the time. But I don’t know if I’d say it was like this.\n\nI’m trying to think back and not exaggerate either way. There were times where I wasn’t in a great place, but usually I could point to a reason and it would pass, or I’d still feel like myself underneath it. This feels more… I don’t know, more stuck, I guess.\n\nSo I don’t think I’ve had a period exactly like this before, not that I can clearly remember. But I also haven’t really sat down and thought about it in this kind of way before, so I’m not completely sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that’s been getting me in trouble at work.\n\nWhen I’m watching the CCTV feeds, I’ll be staring right at the monitors, but my mind just kind of… slips off somewhere. Not even somewhere important, sometimes it’s just blank or random thoughts, and then I realize I wasn’t really taking in what I was looking at for a few seconds or longer. In that job, even a little lapse like that matters.\n\nAnd with incident reports, it’s been frustrating because I’ll write something, then reread it, and I still don’t trust that I actually understood what I wrote. I end up going over the same sentence again and again, or checking details I normally would’ve caught the first time. It feels like my brain isn’t holding onto things the way it usually does.\n\nThat’s how I ended up making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. It’s not that I don’t care or I’m trying to cut corners. I’m trying, but my focus just isn’t staying where it’s supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually energetic or high, needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing.\n\nI mean, I’ve had times where I stayed up late or had a busy stretch and maybe got less sleep because I had things to do, but I wouldn’t say I felt unusually high or full of energy from it.
