But the thought has been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight since this started?\n\nPatient: I don’t know exactly how many pounds, because I haven’t really been checking the scale every day or anything, but I wouldn’t be surprised if I have. The bigger thing I’ve noticed is my appetite’s been really off these last few weeks.\n\nThat’s actually one of the reasons I came in. I’ll get home after a shift and there’ll be food I normally like, stuff I’d usually have no problem eating, and it just kind of sits there in front of me. I’ll take a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it feels like I’m forcing it down more than actually wanting it. So maybe I’ve lost some weight, yeah, but mostly I’ve just barely been eating like I normally do.\n\nDoctor: How have you been sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that in a neat way. I haven’t been keeping track of it like, “I slept this many hours,” or anything. Some nights I’m in bed and it feels like I’ve been lying there a long time, but then I don’t know if I actually slept more than I think I did or less than I think I did.\n\nI guess what I can say is it hasn’t felt very restful. I’ll go to bed because I know I need to, especially with work and everything, but when morning comes I don’t always feel like I got much out of it. But I can’t say exactly how many hours, or whether it’s every single night. It’s just been… off, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still going in. I haven’t stopped showing up for work or anything, and at home I’m still doing the basic things I have to do, even if it takes me longer or I kind of drag myself through it.\n\nBut at work, it’s starting to show in ways that really bother me. My job is the kind where you’re supposed to be paying attention, writing things down correctly, catching things when they happen. And I’ve made some documentation mistakes lately that I normally wouldn’t make. Not huge, dramatic mistakes, but enough that someone noticed. And there was one surveillance cue I missed, which… that’s not like me. I keep replaying it because I know better.\n\nI actually got a formal warning, and that’s probably what made me realize this isn’t just me having a rough few days. I’ve never really had that happen before. I’m usually dependable, I do the job, I keep my head down. So yeah, I’m functioning, technically, but it’s affecting my work now in a way it never used to.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I’ve had bad stretches before, like anyone, where I was down for a few days or stressed out about work or money or whatever was going on at the time. But I don’t know if I’d say it was like this.\n\nI’m trying to think back and not exaggerate either way. There were times where I wasn’t in a great place, but usually I could point to a reason and it would pass, or I’d still feel like myself underneath it. This feels more… I don’t know, more stuck, I guess.\n\nSo I don’t think I’ve had a period exactly like this before, not that I can clearly remember. But I also haven’t really sat down and thought about it in this kind of way before, so I’m not completely sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that’s been getting me in trouble at work.\n\nWhen I’m watching the CCTV feeds, I’ll be staring right at the monitors, but my mind just kind of… slips off somewhere. Not even somewhere important, sometimes it’s just blank or random thoughts, and then I realize I wasn’t really taking in what I was looking at for a few seconds or longer. In that job, even a little lapse like that matters.\n\nAnd with incident reports, it’s been frustrating because I’ll write something, then reread it, and I still don’t trust that I actually understood what I wrote. I end up going over the same sentence again and again, or checking details I normally would’ve caught the first time. It feels like my brain isn’t holding onto things the way it usually does.\n\nThat’s how I ended up making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. It’s not that I don’t care or I’m trying to cut corners. I’m trying, but my focus just isn’t staying where it’s supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually energetic or high, needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing.\n\nI mean, I’ve had times where I stayed up late or had a busy stretch and maybe got less sleep because I had things to do, but I wouldn’t say I felt unusually high or full of energy from it. If anything, when I don’t sleep enough I usually feel it. I get tired, slower, kind of run down.\n\nI’m trying to think if there was ever a time where people would’ve said I was acting different, like really wired or more energetic than usual, and nothing really comes to mind. Not like days where I barely needed sleep and still felt great or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than you would expect just from the changes at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it is stronger than just work stuff. I mean, the changes at work definitely didn’t help, and I can point to certain things there that make me feel worse, but this feels bigger than that.\n\nMost days it’s like I’m carrying this heavy, flat feeling around with me. It’s not just when I’m on shift or dealing with something stressful. Even when I’m home with my family, or just sitting in the break room, or doing nothing in particular, I still feel kind of… not fully there. Like everything is happening around me and I’m present physically, but inside I’m just dulled out.\n\nThat’s what makes me think it’s not only about the job changes. If it were just work, I’d expect maybe I’d feel better once I got home or once I was away from it for a while. But I can’t say exactly how many hours, or whether it’s every single night. I haven’t stopped showing up for work or anything, and at home I’m still doing the basic things I have to do, even if it takes me longer or I kind of drag myself through it.\n\nBut at work, it’s starting to show in ways that really bother me. My job is the kind where you’re supposed to be paying attention, writing things down correctly, catching things when they happen. And I’ve made some documentation mistakes lately that I normally wouldn’t make. Not huge, dramatic mistakes, but enough that someone noticed. And there was one surveillance cue I missed, which… that’s not like me. I keep replaying it because I know better.\n\nI actually got a formal warning, and that’s probably what made me realize this isn’t just me having a rough few days. I’ve never really had that happen before. I’m usually dependable, I do the job, I keep my head down. So yeah, I’m functioning, technically, but it’s affecting my work now in a way it never used to.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I’ve had bad stretches before, like anyone, where I was down for a few days or stressed out about work or money or whatever was going on at the time. But I don’t know if I’d say it was like this.\n\nI’m trying to think back and not exaggerate either way. There were times where I wasn’t in a great place, but usually I could point to a reason and it would pass, or I’d still feel like myself underneath it. This feels more… I don’t know, more stuck, I guess.\n\nSo I don’t think I’ve had a period exactly like this before, not that I can clearly remember. But I also haven’t really sat down and thought about it in this kind of way before, so I’m not completely sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that’s been getting me in trouble at work.\n\nWhen I’m watching the CCTV feeds, I’ll be staring right at the monitors, but my mind just kind of… slips off somewhere. Not even somewhere important, sometimes it’s just blank or random thoughts, and then I realize I wasn’t really taking in what I was looking at for a few seconds or longer. In that job, even a little lapse like that matters.\n\nAnd with incident reports, it’s been frustrating because I’ll write something, then reread it, and I still don’t trust that I actually understood what I wrote. I end up going over the same sentence again and again, or checking details I normally would’ve caught the first time. It feels like my brain isn’t holding onto things the way it usually does.\n\nThat’s how I ended up making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. It’s not that I don’t care or I’m trying to cut corners. I’m trying, but my focus just isn’t staying where it’s supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually energetic or high, needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing.\n\nI mean, I’ve had times where I stayed up late or had a busy stretch and maybe got less sleep because I had things to do, but I wouldn’t say I felt unusually high or full of energy from it. If anything, when I don’t sleep enough I usually feel it. I get tired, slower, kind of run down.\n\nI’m trying to think if there was ever a time where people would’ve said I was acting different, like really wired or more energetic than usual, and nothing really comes to mind. Not like days where I barely needed sleep and still felt great or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than you would expect just from the changes at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it is stronger than just work stuff. I mean, the changes at work definitely didn’t help, and I can point to certain things there that make me feel worse, but this feels bigger than that.\n\nMost days it’s like I’m carrying this heavy, flat feeling around with me. It’s not just when I’m on shift or dealing with something stressful. Even when I’m home with my family, or just sitting in the break room, or doing nothing in particular, I still feel kind of… not fully there. Like everything is happening around me and I’m present physically, but inside I’m just dulled out.\n\nThat’s what makes me think it’s not only about the job changes. If it were just work, I’d expect maybe I’d feel better once I got home or once I was away from it for a while. But a lot of the time the feeling comes with me, and it just sits there.\n\nDoctor: Compared with what changed at work, does your depression feel out of proportion to the actual work stress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it does. I know work has been stressful, and I don’t want to act like that part is nothing, because it does get to me. But this feels heavier than what I’d expect from just schedule changes or pressure at work.\n\nIt’s more like most days I’m carrying this heavy, flat feeling around with me. And it doesn’t really shut off when I leave. I can be home with my family, or just sitting in the break room, and I’m still kind of… not really there. Like I’m there physically, but inside I feel dulled out, like everything is muted.\n\nSo yeah, compared to the actual work stress, it feels out of proportion. It follows me around more than I think normal job stress should.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, like anyone, where I felt down or stressed for a while, but I don’t know if I’d say it was like this or that it clearly lasted two weeks or more.\n\nI’m trying to think back, and nothing stands out in the same way this current period does. There were probably times I wasn’t myself, maybe quieter or more discouraged, but I didn’t really keep track of it, you know? I wasn’t marking days on a calendar or thinking, “This has been going on for two weeks.”\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I don’t know for sure. I can’t point to another definite time that was like this.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, was there ever a time in your life when you felt depressed or lost interest in things nearly every day for two weeks or longer?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I’ve been trying to think about that, and I can remember times where I was down or stressed or kind of not myself, but I can’t really say there was a clear period before this where it was nearly every day for two weeks or longer.\n\nLike, maybe there were stretches where I was quieter or less interested in doing things, but I didn’t really think of it that way at the time. I wasn’t paying attention to how many days it lasted or whether it was every day. So I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nNothing before this really stands out to me as being clearly like what’s going on now.\n\nDoctor: Besides the changes at work, has anything else stressful happened recently?\n\nPatient: Not really… at least nothing that I can point to and say, “That’s what set this off.”\n\nI mean, normal life stuff is always there — bills, family responsibilities, little things around the house, trying to keep up with everything — but I can’t think of some big separate stressful event recently besides the work changes. Nothing major happened that I’m aware of, no big crisis or anything like that.\n\nI’ve gone over it in my head a lot, actually, because I keep wondering if I’m missing something obvious. But the only thing that really changed recently was work. The rest is just the usual background stress, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Before this current episode, was there ever a time in your life when you felt depressed or lost interest in things nearly every day for at least two months?\n\nPatient: Two months? I don’t think so… I mean, I’m trying to really look back and be fair about it, because two months is a long stretch, and I feel like I would probably remember if I was feeling that low or that uninterested in things almost every day for that long.\n\nI’ve had times where I was in a bad mood, or where work or life got to me, and maybe I wasn’t as upbeat or I kept more to myself for a little while. But before this current thing, I can’t point to a period where it was nearly every day for at least two months. Nothing that clear comes to mind.\n\nSo I guess my answer is no, not that I’m aware of… or at least I don’t remember anything like that lasting that long before.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I do think about work, obviously, because it’s been on my mind, but I don’t know if I’d say it’s over and over for a lot of the day in that way.

That’s actually been one of the weirder parts, because I’m not someone who usually has a hard time filling my time. I used to really look forward to going for a run in the morning — nothing intense, just getting out before the day started, clearing my head a little. Lately I’ll set my shoes out or think, “Okay, tomorrow I’m going,” and then when the time comes it just feels like this huge chore, like I can’t make myself care enough to do it.\n\nSame with playing guitar. I’ve always kept it nearby, and normally I’d pick it up without even thinking about it, just mess around for a while. Now it kind of just sits there. I look at it and think I should play, but there’s no pull toward it, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd cooking dinner too — I used to actually enjoy that, planning what I was going to make, getting it started after work. Now it’s like every step feels like too much, and not in a busy way, more like… I just don’t get anything out of it. It all feels like another task I’m supposed to force myself through.\n\nDoctor: About how long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’d say it’s been about three weeks now. It kind of started after they reorganized the security operations at the casino and changed around my assignments. At first I thought I was just reacting to the schedule and the different routine, you know, like maybe I just needed a few days to adjust.\n\nBut it didn’t really lift. It’s been pretty steady since then — not every single minute is exactly the same, but overall, yeah, about three weeks of feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this started because of the changes at work?\n\nPatient: I think so, yeah. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like one thing at work magically caused everything, but the timing lines up pretty clearly in my head. It’s been going on about three weeks, and that’s right around when the casino reorganized the security operations and changed my assignments.\n\nAt first I honestly thought I was just thrown off by the change — different responsibilities, different rhythm to the day, having to adjust to how things were being handled. I figured maybe after a few shifts I’d settle in and it would pass. But it didn’t really pass. It just kind of stayed with me. So yeah, I’d say it started after those changes at work, or at least that’s when I noticed it beginning.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d use the word hopeless exactly every time, but most days there’s this heavy, flat feeling that I’m carrying around. Like I can be at home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work, and I’m physically there, I’m answering people, I’m going through the motions, but inside it feels like I’m not really there.\n\nIt’s not always some dramatic thought like “nothing will ever get better.” It’s more like everything feels dulled out and hard to reach, if that makes sense. Like I’m looking at my normal life from a distance and I can’t quite get back into it. That part has been pretty unsettling.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of wanting to die or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I haven’t made any plans to hurt myself or anything like that. I don’t… I don’t want to scare anybody by saying it wrong.\n\nBut yeah, I have had thoughts where I think, like, if I just didn’t wake up in the morning, maybe that wouldn’t be the worst thing. Not that I’m trying to make that happen, and I’m not sitting there figuring out how to do something to myself. It’s more like when I’m really tired of pushing through the day, this thought comes in that maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t have to keep doing this.\n\nIt’s hard to admit that out loud, because I don’t want it to sound like I’m about to do something. I’m not. But the thought has been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight since this started?\n\nPatient: I don’t know exactly how many pounds, because I haven’t really been checking the scale every day or anything, but I wouldn’t be surprised if I have. The bigger thing I’ve noticed is my appetite’s been really off these last few weeks.\n\nThat’s actually one of the reasons I came in. I’ll get home after a shift and there’ll be food I normally like, stuff I’d usually have no problem eating, and it just kind of sits there in front of me. I’ll take a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it feels like I’m forcing it down more than actually wanting it. So maybe I’ve lost some weight, yeah, but mostly I’ve just barely been eating like I normally do.\n\nDoctor: How have you been sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that in a neat way. I haven’t been keeping track of it like, “I slept this many hours,” or anything. Some nights I’m in bed and it feels like I’ve been lying there a long time, but then I don’t know if I actually slept more than I think I did or less than I think I did.\n\nI guess what I can say is it hasn’t felt very restful. I’ll go to bed because I know I need to, especially with work and everything, but when morning comes I don’t always feel like I got much out of it. But I can’t say exactly how many hours, or whether it’s every single night. It’s just been… off, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still going in. I haven’t stopped showing up for work or anything, and at home I’m still doing the basic things I have to do, even if it takes me longer or I kind of drag myself through it.\n\nBut at work, it’s starting to show in ways that really bother me. My job is the kind where you’re supposed to be paying attention, writing things down correctly, catching things when they happen. And I’ve made some documentation mistakes lately that I normally wouldn’t make. Not huge, dramatic mistakes, but enough that someone noticed. And there was one surveillance cue I missed, which… that’s not like me. I keep replaying it because I know better.\n\nI actually got a formal warning, and that’s probably what made me realize this isn’t just me having a rough few days. I’ve never really had that happen before. I’m usually dependable, I do the job, I keep my head down. So yeah, I’m functioning, technically, but it’s affecting my work now in a way it never used to.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I’ve had bad stretches before, like anyone, where I was down for a few days or stressed out about work or money or whatever was going on at the time. But I don’t know if I’d say it was like this.\n\nI’m trying to think back and not exaggerate either way. There were times where I wasn’t in a great place, but usually I could point to a reason and it would pass, or I’d still feel like myself underneath it. This feels more… I don’t know, more stuck, I guess.\n\nSo I don’t think I’ve had a period exactly like this before, not that I can clearly remember. But I also haven’t really sat down and thought about it in this kind of way before, so I’m not completely sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that’s been getting me in trouble at work.\n\nWhen I’m watching the CCTV feeds, I’ll be staring right at the monitors, but my mind just kind of… slips off somewhere. Not even somewhere important, sometimes it’s just blank or random thoughts, and then I realize I wasn’t really taking in what I was looking at for a few seconds or longer. In that job, even a little lapse like that matters.\n\nAnd with incident reports, it’s been frustrating because I’ll write something, then reread it, and I still don’t trust that I actually understood what I wrote. I end up going over the same sentence again and again, or checking details I normally would’ve caught the first time. It feels like my brain isn’t holding onto things the way it usually does.\n\nThat’s how I ended up making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. It’s not that I don’t care or I’m trying to cut corners. I’m trying, but my focus just isn’t staying where it’s supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually energetic or high, needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing.\n\nI mean, I’ve had times where I stayed up late or had a busy stretch and maybe got less sleep because I had things to do, but I wouldn’t say I felt unusually high or full of energy from it. If anything, when I don’t sleep enough I usually feel it. I get tired, slower, kind of run down.\n\nI’m trying to think if there was ever a time where people would’ve said I was acting different, like really wired or more energetic than usual, and nothing really comes to mind. Not like days where I barely needed sleep and still felt great or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than you would expect just from the changes at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it is stronger than just work stuff. I mean, the changes at work definitely didn’t help, and I can point to certain things there that make me feel worse, but this feels bigger than that.\n\nMost days it’s like I’m carrying this heavy, flat feeling around with me. It’s not just when I’m on shift or dealing with something stressful. Even when I’m home with my family, or just sitting in the break room, or doing nothing in particular, I still feel kind of… not fully there. Like everything is happening around me and I’m present physically, but inside I’m just dulled out.\n\nThat’s what makes me think it’s not only about the job changes. If it were just work, I’d expect maybe I’d feel better once I got home or once I was away from it for a while. But a lot of the time the feeling comes with me, and it just sits there.\n\nDoctor: Compared with what changed at work, does your depression feel out of proportion to the actual work stress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it does. I know work has been stressful, and I don’t want to act like that part is nothing, because it does get to me. But this feels heavier than what I’d expect from just schedule changes or pressure at work.\n\nIt’s more like most days I’m carrying this heavy, flat feeling around with me. And it doesn’t really shut off when I leave. I can be home with my family, or just sitting in the break room, and I’m still kind of… not really there. Like I’m there physically, but inside I feel dulled out, like everything is muted.\n\nSo yeah, compared to the actual work stress, it feels out of proportion. It follows me around more than I think normal job stress should.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, like anyone, where I felt down or stressed for a while, but I don’t know if I’d say it was like this or that it clearly lasted two weeks or more.\n\nI’m trying to think back, and nothing stands out in the same way this current period does. There were probably times I wasn’t myself, maybe quieter or more discouraged, but I didn’t really keep track of it, you know? I wasn’t marking days on a calendar or thinking, “This has been going on for two weeks.”\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I don’t know for sure. I can’t point to another definite time that was like this.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, was there ever a time in your life when you felt depressed or lost interest in things nearly every day for two weeks or longer?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I’ve been trying to think about that, and I can remember times where I was down or stressed or kind of not myself, but I can’t really say there was a clear period before this where it was nearly every day for two weeks or longer.\n\nLike, maybe there were stretches where I was quieter or less interested in doing things, but I didn’t really think of it that way at the time. I wasn’t paying attention to how many days it lasted or whether it was every day. So I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nNothing before this really stands out to me as being clearly like what’s going on now.\n\nDoctor: Besides the changes at work, has anything else stressful happened recently?\n\nPatient: Not really… at least nothing that I can point to and say, “That’s what set this off.”\n\nI mean, normal life stuff is always there — bills, family responsibilities, little things around the house, trying to keep up with everything — but I can’t think of some big separate stressful event recently besides the work changes. Nothing major happened that I’m aware of, no big crisis or anything like that.\n\nI’ve gone over it in my head a lot, actually, because I keep wondering if I’m missing something obvious. But the only thing that really changed recently was work. The rest is just the usual background stress, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Before this current episode, was there ever a time in your life when you felt depressed or lost interest in things nearly every day for at least two months?\n\nPatient: Two months? I don’t think so… I mean, I’m trying to really look back and be fair about it, because two months is a long stretch, and I feel like I would probably remember if I was feeling that low or that uninterested in things almost every day for that long.\n\nI’ve had times where I was in a bad mood, or where work or life got to me, and maybe I wasn’t as upbeat or I kept more to myself for a little while. But before this current thing, I can’t point to a period where it was nearly every day for at least two months. Nothing that clear comes to mind.\n\nSo I guess my answer is no, not that I’m aware of… or at least I don’t remember anything like that lasting that long before.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about the changes at work over and over a lot of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I do think about work, obviously, because it’s been on my mind, but I don’t know if I’d say it’s over and over for a lot of the day in that way.\n\nSometimes something will remind me of it, or I’ll catch myself thinking about how things used to be versus how they are now, and then I’ll kind of go down that road for a bit. But I haven’t really been tracking it, so it’s hard for me to say, like, “yes, it’s most of the day” or anything definite like that.\n\nIt’s more that it’s there in the background at times, I guess. I don’t know if that makes sense. I wouldn’t want to exaggerate it, but I also wouldn’t say I never think about it."
